0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;First, an agent needs a browser. With Browser Run, agents can spin up a headless Chrome instance on Cloudflareâs global network, on demand. No infrastructure to manage, no Chrome versions to maintain. Browser sessions open near users for low latency, and scale up and down as needed. Pair Browser Run with the &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/agents/api-reference/browse-the-web/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Agents SDK&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; to build long-running agents that browse the web, remember everything, and act on their own.Â &lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h2 id=\&quot;2-take-actions\&quot;&gt;2) Take actions&lt;/h2&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#2-take-actions\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;Once your agent has a browser, it needs ways to control it. Browser Run supports multiple approaches: new low-level protocol access with the Chrome DevTools Protocol (CDP) and WebMCP, in addition to existing higher-level automation using &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/browser-rendering/puppeteer/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Puppeteer&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; and &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/browser-rendering/playwright/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Playwright&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;, and &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/browser-rendering/rest-api/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Quick Actions&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; for simple tasks. Letâs look at the details.&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h3 id=\&quot;chrome-devtools-protocol-cdp-endpoint\&quot;&gt;Chrome DevTools Protocol (CDP) endpoint&lt;/h3&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#chrome-devtools-protocol-cdp-endpoint\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;The &lt;a href=\&quot;https://chromedevtools.github.io/devtools-protocol/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Chrome DevTools Protocol (CDP)&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; is the low-level protocol that powers browser automation. Exposing CDP directly means the growing ecosystem of agent tools and existing CDP automation scripts can use Browser Run. When you open Chrome DevTools and inspect a page, CDP is what&amp;#39;s running underneath. Puppeteer, Playwright, and most agent frameworks are built on top of it.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Every way that you have been using Browser Run has actually been through CDP already. Whatâs new is that we&amp;#39;re now &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/browser-rendering/cdp/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;exposing CDP directly&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; as an endpoint. This matters for agents because CDP gives agents the most control possible over the browser. Agent frameworks already speak CDP natively, and can now connect to Browser Run directly. CDP also unlocks browser actions that aren&amp;#39;t available through Puppeteer or Playwright, like JavaScript debugging. And because you&amp;#39;re working with raw CDP messages instead of going through higher-level libraries, you can pass messages directly to models for more token-efficient browser control.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;If you already have CDP automation scripts running against self-hosted Chrome, they work on Browser Run with a one-line config change. Point your WebSocket URL at Browser Run and stop managing your own browser infrastructure.&lt;/p&gt;\n            &lt;pre class=\&quot;language-javascript\&quot;&gt;&lt;code class=\&quot;language-javascript\&quot;&gt;// Before: connecting to self-hosted Chrome\nconst browser = await puppeteer.connect({\n  browserWSEndpoint: &amp;#039;ws://localhost:9222/devtools/browser&amp;#039;\n});\n\n// After: connecting to Browser Run\nconst browser = await puppeteer.connect({\n  browserWSEndpoint: &amp;#039;wss://api.cloudflare.com/client/v4/accounts/&amp;lt;ACCOUNT_ID&amp;gt;/browser-rendering/devtools/browser&amp;#039;,\n  headers: { &amp;#039;Authorization&amp;#039;: &amp;#039;Bearer &amp;lt;API_TOKEN&amp;gt;&amp;#039; }\n});\n&lt;/pre&gt;&lt;/code&gt;\n            &lt;p&gt;The CDP endpoint also makes Browser Run more accessible. You can now connect from any language, any environment, without needing to write a &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/workers/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Cloudflare Worker&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;. (If you&amp;#39;re already using Workers, nothing changes.)&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h4 id=\&quot;using-browser-run-with-mcp-clients\&quot;&gt;Using Browser Run with MCP Clients&lt;/h4&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#using-browser-run-with-mcp-clients\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;Now that Browser Run exposes the Chrome DevTools Protocol (CDP), MCP clients including Claude Desktop, Cursor, Codex, and OpenCode can use Browser Run as their remote browser. The &lt;a href=\&quot;https://github.com/ChromeDevTools/chrome-devtools-mcp\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;chrome-devtools-mcp package&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; from the Chrome DevTools team is an MCP server that gives your AI coding assistant access to the full power of Chrome DevTools for reliable automation, in-depth debugging, and performance analysis.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Hereâs an example of how to configure Browser Run for Claude Desktop:&lt;/p&gt;\n            &lt;pre class=\&quot;language-javascript\&quot;&gt;&lt;code class=\&quot;language-javascript\&quot;&gt;{\n  &amp;quot;mcpServers&amp;quot;: {\n    &amp;quot;browser-rendering&amp;quot;: {\n      &amp;quot;command&amp;quot;: &amp;quot;npx&amp;quot;,\n      &amp;quot;args&amp;quot;: [\n        &amp;quot;-y&amp;quot;,\n        &amp;quot;chrome-devtools-mcp@latest&amp;quot;,\n        &amp;quot;--wsEndpoint=wss://api.cloudflare.com/client/v4/accounts/&amp;lt;ACCOUNT_ID&amp;gt;/browser-rendering/devtools/browser?keep_alive=600000&amp;quot;,\n        &amp;quot;--wsHeaders={\\&amp;quot;Authorization\\&amp;quot;:\\&amp;quot;Bearer &amp;lt;API_TOKEN&amp;gt;\\&amp;quot;}&amp;quot;\n      ]\n    }\n  }\n}\n&lt;/pre&gt;&lt;/code&gt;\n            &lt;p&gt;For other MCP clients, see &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/browser-rendering/cdp/mcp-clients/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;documentation for using Browser Run with MCP clients&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h3 id=\&quot;webmcp-support\&quot;&gt;WebMCP support&lt;/h3&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#webmcp-support\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;The Internet was built for humans, so navigating as an AI agent today is unreliable. Weâre betting on a future where more agents use the web than humans. In that world, sites need to be agent-friendly.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Thatâs why weâre launching support for &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developer.chrome.com/blog/webmcp-epp\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;WebMCP&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;, a new browser API from the Google Chrome team that landed in Chromium 146+. WebMCP lets websites expose tools directly to AI agents, declaring what actions are available for agents to discover and call on each page. This helps agents navigate the web more reliably. Instead of agents needing to figure out how to use a site, websites can expose their tools for agents to discover and call&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Two APIs make this work:&lt;/p&gt;&lt;ul&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;code&gt;navigator.modelContext&lt;/code&gt; allows websites to register their tools&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;code&gt;navigator.modelContextTesting&lt;/code&gt; allows agents to discover and execute those tools&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;/ul&gt;&lt;p&gt;Today, an agent visiting a travel booking site has to figure out the UI by looking at it. With WebMCP, the site declares âhereâs a search_flights tool that takes an origin, destination, and date.â The agent calls it directly, without having to loop through slow screenshot-analyze-click loops. This makes navigation more reliable regardless of potential changes to the UI.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Tools are discovered on the page rather than preloaded. This matters for the long tail of the web, where preloading an MCP server for every possible site is not feasible and would bloat the context window.Â &lt;/p&gt;&lt;div style=\&quot;position: relative; padding-top: 62.7177700348432%;\&quot;&gt;\n  &lt;iframe\n    src=\&quot;https://customer-eq7kiuol0tk9chox.cloudflarestream.com/cdef25a5448e8f745c319974849a74d9/iframe?preload=true&amp;poster=https%3A%2F%2Fcustomer-eq7kiuol0tk9chox.cloudflarestream.com%2Fcdef25a5448e8f745c319974849a74d9%2Fthumbnails%2Fthumbnail.jpg%3Ftime%3D%26height%3D600\&quot;\n    loading=\&quot;lazy\&quot;\n    style=\&quot;border: none; position: absolute; top: 0; left: 0; height: 100%; width: 100%;\&quot;\n    allow=\&quot;accelerometer; gyroscope; autoplay; encrypted-media; picture-in-picture;\&quot;\n    allowfullscreen=\&quot;true\&quot;\n  &gt;&lt;/iframe&gt;\n&lt;/div&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;sup&gt;&lt;i&gt;Using WebMCP to book a hotel through the Chrome DevTools console, discovering available tools with listTools()&lt;/i&gt;&lt;/sup&gt;&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;We have an experimental pool with browser instances running Chrome beta so you can test emerging browser features before they reach stable Chrome. We also just shipped &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/browser-rendering/reference/wrangler-commands/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Wrangler browser commands&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; that let you manage browser sessions directly from the CLI, letting you create, manage, and view browser sessions directly from your terminal. To &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/browser-run/features/webmcp/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;access WebMCP-enabled browsers&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;, use the following Wrangler command to create a session in the experimental pool:&lt;/p&gt;\n            &lt;pre class=\&quot;language-javascript\&quot;&gt;&lt;code class=\&quot;language-javascript\&quot;&gt;npm i -g wrangler@latest\nwrangler browser create --lab --keepAlive 300  \n&lt;/pre&gt;&lt;/code&gt;\n            \n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h3 id=\&quot;existing-ways-to-use-browser-run\&quot;&gt;Existing ways to use Browser Run&lt;/h3&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#existing-ways-to-use-browser-run\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;While CDP and WebMCP are new, you could already use &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/browser-rendering/puppeteer/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Puppeteer&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;, &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/browser-rendering/playwright/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Playwright&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;, or &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/browser-rendering/stagehand/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; And for simple tasks like &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/browser-rendering/rest-api/screenshot-endpoint/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;capturing screenshots&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;, &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/browser-rendering/rest-api/pdf-endpoint/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Quick Action endpoints&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;.Â &lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h4 id=\&quot;crawl-endpoint-crawl-web-content\&quot;&gt;/crawl endpoint â crawl web content&lt;/h4&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#crawl-endpoint-crawl-web-content\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;/crawl endpoint&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; that lets you crawl entire sites with a single API call. Give it a starting URL and pages are automatically discovered and scraped, then returned in your preferred format (HTML, Markdown, and structured JSON), with additional parameters to control crawl depth and scope, skip pages that havenât changed, and specify certain paths to include or exclude.Â &lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;We intentionally built /crawl to be a &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/browser-run/faq/#will-browser-run-be-detected-by-bot-management\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;well-behaved crawler&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;. That means it respects site ownerâs preferences out of the box, is a &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/bots/concepts/bot/signed-agents/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;signed agent&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; with a distinct bot ID that is cryptographically signed using &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/bots/reference/bot-verification/web-bot-auth/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Web Bot Auth&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;, a non-customizable &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/browser-rendering/rest-api/crawl-endpoint/#user-agent\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;User-Agent&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;, and follows robots.txt and &lt;a href=\&quot;https://www.cloudflare.com/ai-crawl-control/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;AI Crawl Control&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;. It does not bypass Cloudflareâs bot protections or CAPTCHAs. Site owners choose whether their content is accessible and /crawl respects it.Â &lt;/p&gt;\n            &lt;pre class=\&quot;language-javascript\&quot;&gt;&lt;code class=\&quot;language-javascript\&quot;&gt;# Initiate a crawl\ncurl -X POST &amp;#039;https://api.cloudflare.com/client/v4/accounts/{account_id}/browser-rendering/crawl&amp;#039; \\\n  -H &amp;#039;Authorization: Bearer &amp;lt;apiToken&amp;gt;&amp;#039; \\\n  -H &amp;#039;Content-Type: application/json&amp;#039; \\\n  -d &amp;#039;{\n    &amp;quot;url&amp;quot;: &amp;quot;https://blog.cloudflare.com/&amp;quot;\n  }&amp;#039;\n&lt;/pre&gt;&lt;/code&gt;\n            \n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h2 id=\&quot;3-observe\&quot;&gt;3) Observe&lt;/h2&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#3-observe\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;Things donât always go right the first try. Using sandboxed TypeScript, it helps you troubleshoot and manage your stack as a grounded technical collaborator.\n&quot;],&quot;featured&quot;:[0,false],&quot;html&quot;:[0,&quot;&lt;p&gt;While there have been small improvements along the way, the interface of technical products has not really changed since the dawn of the Internet. It still remains: clicking five pages deep, cross-referencing logs across tabs, and hunting for hidden toggles.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;AI gives us the opportunity to rethink all that. Instead of complexity spread over a sprawling graphical user interface: what if you could describe in plain language what you wanted to achieve?Â &lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;This is the future â and weâre launching it today. We didnât want to just put an agent in a dashboard. We wanted to create an entirely new way to interact with our entire platform. Any task, any surface, a single prompt.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Introducing Agent Lee.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Agent Lee is an in-dashboard AI assistant that understands &lt;b&gt;your&lt;/b&gt; Cloudflare account.Â &lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;It can help you with troubleshooting, which, today, is a manual grind. If your Worker starts returning 503s at 02:00 UTC, finding the root cause:Â be it an R2 bucket, a misconfigured route, or a hidden rate limit, youâre opening half a dozen tabs and hoping you recognize the pattern. Most developers don&amp;#39;t have a teammate who knows the entire platform standing over their shoulder at 2 a.m. Agent Lee does.Â &lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;But it wonât just troubleshoot for you at 2 a.m. Agent Lee will also fix the problem for you on the spot.&lt;/p&gt;\n          &lt;figure class=\&quot;kg-card kg-image-card\&quot;&gt;\n          &lt;Image src=\&quot;https://cf-assets.www.cloudflare.com/zkvhlag99gkb/2Iva79HIiHPUrK8NLukkwH/dd1cf1709ab04f6d5825124cecd20a5e/BLOG-3231_2.png\&quot; alt=\&quot;BLOG-3231 2\&quot; class=\&quot;kg-image\&quot; width=\&quot;1999\&quot; height=\&quot;1013\&quot; loading=\&quot;lazy\&quot;/&gt;\n          &lt;/figure&gt;&lt;p&gt;Agent Lee has been running in an active beta during which it has served over 18,000 daily users, executing nearly a quarter of a million tool calls per day. While we are confident in its current capabilities and success in production, this is a system we are continuously developing. As it remains in beta, you may encounter unexpected limitations or edge cases as we refine its performance. We encourage you to use the feedback form below to help us make it better every day.&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h2 id=\&quot;what-agent-lee-can-do\&quot;&gt;What Agent Lee can do&lt;/h2&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#what-agent-lee-can-do\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;Agent Lee is built directly into the dashboard and understands the resources in your account. It knows your Workers, your zones, your DNS configuration, your error rates. The knowledge that today lives across six tabs and two browser windows will now live in one place, and you can talk to it.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;With natural language, you can use it to:&lt;/p&gt;&lt;ul&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;Answer questions about your account:&lt;/b&gt; &amp;quot;Show me the top 5 error messages on my Worker.&amp;quot;&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;Debug an issue:&lt;/b&gt; &amp;quot;I can&amp;#39;t access my site with the www prefix.&amp;quot;&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;Apply a change:&lt;/b&gt; &amp;quot;Enable Access for my domain.&amp;quot;&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;Deploy a resource: &lt;/b&gt;&amp;quot;Create a new R2 bucket for my photos and connect it to my Worker.&amp;quot;&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;/ul&gt;&lt;p&gt;Instead of switching between products, you describe what you want to do, and Agent Lee helps you get there with instructions and visualizations. It retrieves context, uses the right tools, and creates dynamic visualizations based on the types of questions you ask. Ask what your error rate looks like over the last 24 hours, and it renders a chart inline, pulling from your actual traffic, not sending you to a separate Analytics page.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;div style=\&quot;position: relative; padding-top: 44.08713692946058%;\&quot;&gt;\n  &lt;iframe\n    src=\&quot;https://customer-eq7kiuol0tk9chox.cloudflarestream.com/16f8e9c688d9493c1ddb124e0d597d43/iframe?poster=https%3A%2F%2Fcustomer-eq7kiuol0tk9chox.cloudflarestream.com%2F16f8e9c688d9493c1ddb124e0d597d43%2Fthumbnails%2Fthumbnail.jpg%3Ftime%3D%26height%3D600\&quot;\n    loading=\&quot;lazy\&quot;\n    style=\&quot;border: none; position: absolute; top: 0; left: 0; height: 100%; width: 100%;\&quot;\n    allow=\&quot;accelerometer; gyroscope; autoplay; encrypted-media; picture-in-picture;\&quot;\n    allowfullscreen=\&quot;true\&quot;\n  &gt;&lt;/iframe&gt;\n&lt;/div&gt;&lt;p&gt;Agent Lee isn&amp;#39;t answering FAQ questions â it&amp;#39;s doing real work, against real accounts, at scale. aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;Rather than presenting MCP tool definitions directly to the model, Agent Lee uses &lt;a href=\&quot;https://blog.cloudflare.com/code-mode/\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Codemode&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; to convert the tools into a TypeScript API and asks the model to write code that calls it instead.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;This works better for a couple of reasons. LLMs have seen a huge amount of real-world TypeScript but very few tool call examples, so they&amp;#39;re more accurate when working in code. For multi-step tasks, the model can also chain calls together in a single script and return only the final result, ultimately skipping the round-trips.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;The generated code is sent to an upstream Cloudflare MCP server for sandboxed execution, but it goes through a Durable Object that acts as a credentialed proxy. Before any call goes out, the DO classifies the generated code as read or write by inspecting the method and body. Read operations are proxied directly. Write operations are blocked until you explicitly approve them through the elicitation gate. API keys are never present in the generated code â they&amp;#39;re held inside the DO and injected server-side when the upstream call is made. The security boundary isn&amp;#39;t just a sandbox that gets thrown away; it&amp;#39;s a permission architecture that structurally prevents writes from happening without your approval.&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h3 id=\&quot;the-mcp-permission-system\&quot;&gt;The MCP permission system&lt;/h3&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#the-mcp-permission-system\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;Agent Lee connects to Cloudflare&amp;#39;s own MCP server, which exposes two tools: a search tool for querying API endpoints and an execute tool for writing code that performs API requests. This is the surface through which Agent Lee reads your account and, when you approve, writes to it.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Write operations go through an elicitation system that surfaces the approval step before any code executes. Agent Lee cannot skip this step. The permission model is the enforcement layer, and the confirmation prompt you see is not a UX courtesy. It&amp;#39;s the gate.&lt;/p&gt;\n          &lt;figure class=\&quot;kg-card kg-image-card\&quot;&gt;\n          &lt;Image src=\&quot;https://cf-assets.www.cloudflare.com/zkvhlag99gkb/s8phQottGj8yVgc42Nzvl/3abb536756e10360b68cabc0522bcb30/BLOG-3231_4.png\&quot; alt=\&quot;BLOG-3231 4\&quot; class=\&quot;kg-image\&quot; width=\&quot;1608\&quot; height=\&quot;1378\&quot; loading=\&quot;lazy\&quot;/&gt;\n          &lt;/figure&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h2 id=\&quot;built-on-the-same-stack-you-can-use\&quot;&gt;Built on the same stack you can use&lt;/h2&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#built-on-the-same-stack-you-can-use\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;Every primitive Agent Lee is built on is available to all our customers: &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/agents/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Agents SDK&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;, &lt;a href=\&quot;https://www.cloudflare.com/developer-platform/products/workers-ai/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Workers AI&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;, &lt;a href=\&quot;https://www.cloudflare.com/developer-platform/products/durable-objects/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Durable Objects&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;, and the same MCP infrastructure available to any Cloudflare developer. We didn&amp;#39;t build internal tools that aren&amp;#39;t available to you â instead we built it with the same Cloudflare lego blocks that you have access to.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Building Agent Lee on our own primitives wasn&amp;#39;t just a design principle. It was the fastest way to find out what works and what doesn&amp;#39;t. We built this in production, with real users, against real accounts. That means every limitation we hit is a limitation we can fix in the platform. Every pattern that works is one we can make easier for the next team that builds on top of it.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;These are not opinions. They&amp;#39;re what quarter of a million tool calls across 18,000 users a day are telling us.&lt;/p&gt;\n          &lt;figure class=\&quot;kg-card kg-image-card\&quot;&gt;\n          &lt;Image src=\&quot;https://cf-assets.www.cloudflare.com/zkvhlag99gkb/6877BA4kZUUP6qTs5ONucr/50d572b38fecdb3e77ab38d7976f06ed/image5.png\&quot; alt=\&quot;\&quot; class=\&quot;kg-image\&quot; width=\&quot;1999\&quot; height=\&quot;1135\&quot; loading=\&quot;lazy\&quot;/&gt;\n          &lt;/figure&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h2 id=\&quot;generative-ui\&quot;&gt;Generative UI&lt;/h2&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#generative-ui\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;Interacting with a platform should feel like collaborating with an expert. Conversations should transcend simple text. With Agent Lee, as your dialogue evolves, the platform dynamically generates UI components alongside textual responses to provide a richer, more actionable experience.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;For example, if you ask about website traffic trends for the month, you wonât just get a paragraph of numbers. Agent Lee will render an interactive line graph, allowing you to visualize peaks and troughs in activity at a glance.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;To give you full creative control, every conversation is accompanied within an adaptive grid. Here you can click and drag across the grid to carve out space for new UI blocks, then simply describe what you want to see and let the agent handle the heavy lifting.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Today, we support a diverse library of visual blocks, including dynamic tables, interactive charts, architecture maps, and more. By blending the flexibility of natural language with the clarity of structured UI, Agent Lee transforms your chat history into a living dashboard.&lt;/p&gt;\n          &lt;figure class=\&quot;kg-card kg-image-card\&quot;&gt;\n          &lt;Image src=\&quot;https://cf-assets.www.cloudflare.com/zkvhlag99gkb/1oTLXzK5eYGyJm6Y54cKbz/98bae92dc7523f63ac6515d8088e70f7/image4.png\&quot; alt=\&quot;\&quot; class=\&quot;kg-image\&quot; width=\&quot;1999\&quot; height=\&quot;1032\&quot; loading=\&quot;lazy\&quot;/&gt;\n          &lt;/figure&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h2 id=\&quot;measuring-quality-and-safety\&quot;&gt;Measuring quality and safety&lt;/h2&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#measuring-quality-and-safety\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;An agent that can take action on your account needs to be reliable and secure. Elicitations allow agentic systems to actively solicit information, preferences, or approvals from users or other systems mid-execution. When Agent Lee needs to take non-read actions on a user&amp;#39;s behalf we use elicitations by requiring an explicit approval action in the user interface. These guardrails allow Agent Lee to truly be a partner alongside you in managing your resource safely.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;In addition to safety, we continuously measure quality.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;ul&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;Evals to measure conversation success rate and information accuracy.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;Feedback signals from user interactions (thumbs up / thumbs down).&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;Tool call execution success rate and hallucination scorers.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;Per-product breakdown of conversation performance.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;/ul&gt;&lt;p&gt;These systems help us improve Agent Lee over time while keeping users in control.Â &lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h2 id=\&quot;our-vision-ahead\&quot;&gt;Our vision ahead&lt;/h2&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#our-vision-ahead\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;Agent Lee in the dashboard is only the beginning.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;The bigger vision is Agent Lee as the interface to the entire Cloudflare platform â from anywhere. The dashboard today, the CLI next, your phone when you&amp;#39;re on the go. The surface you use shouldn&amp;#39;t matter. You should be able to describe what you need and have it done, regardless of where you are.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;From there, Agent Lee gets proactive. Rather than waiting to be asked, it watches what matters to you, your Workers, your traffic, your error thresholds and reaches out when something warrants attention. An agent that only responds is useful. One that notices things first is something different.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Underlying all of this is context. Agent Lee already knows your account configuration. Over time, it will know more, what you&amp;#39;ve asked before, what page you&amp;#39;re on, what you were debugging last week. That accumulated context is what makes a platform feel less like a tool and more like a collaborator.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;We&amp;#39;re not there yet. Agent Lee today is the first step, running in production, doing real work at scale. The architecture is built to get to the rest. &lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h2 id=\&quot;try-it-out\&quot;&gt;Try it out&lt;/h2&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#try-it-out\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;Agent Lee is available in beta for Free plan users. a new interface to the Cloudflare stack&quot;],&quot;description&quot;:[0,&quot;Agent Lee is an in-dashboard agent that shifts Cloudflareâs interface from manual tab-switching to a single prompt. Using sandboxed TypeScript, it helps you troubleshoot and manage your stack as a grounded technical collaborator.\n&quot;],&quot;imgPreview&quot;:[0,&quot;https://cf-assets.www.cloudflare.com/zkvhlag99gkb/29iboLzcmSDBSB70jCzl30/92685abd0d30671d0ddcae033f9903d8/BLOG-3231_OG.png&quot;]}],&quot;publicly_index&quot;:[0,true]}],[0,{&quot;id&quot;:[0,&quot;3r2ykMs0LTSPwVHmVWldCy&quot;],&quot;title&quot;:[0,&quot;Project Think: building the next generation of AI agents on Cloudflare&quot;],&quot;slug&quot;:[0,&quot;project-think&quot;],&quot;excerpt&quot;:[0,&quot;Announcing a preview of the next edition of the Agents SDK â from lightweight primitives to a batteries-included platform for AI agents that think, act, and persist.\n&quot;],&quot;featured&quot;:[0,true],&quot;html&quot;:[0,&quot;&lt;p&gt;Today, we&amp;#39;re introducing Project Think: the next generation of the &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/agents/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Agents SDK&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;. Project Think is a set of new primitives for building long-running agents (durable execution, sub-agents, sandboxed code execution, persistent sessions) and an opinionated base class that wires them all together. Use the primitives to build exactly what you need, or use the base class to get started fast.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Something happened earlier this year that changed how we think about AI. Tools like &lt;a href=\&quot;https://github.com/badlogic/pi-mono\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Pi&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;, &lt;a href=\&quot;https://github.com/openclaw\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;OpenClaw&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;, &lt;a href=\&quot;https://docs.anthropic.com/en/docs/agents\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Claude Code&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;, and &lt;a href=\&quot;https://openai.com/codex\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Codex&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; proved a simple but powerful idea: give an LLM the ability to read files, write code, execute it, and remember what it learned, and you get something that looks less like a developer tool and more like a general-purpose assistant.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;These coding agents aren&amp;#39;t just writing code anymore. People are using them to manage calendars, analyze datasets, negotiate purchases, file taxes, and automate entire business workflows. The pattern is always the same: the agent reads context, reasons about it, writes code to take action, observes the result, and iterates. Code is the universal medium of action.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Our team has been using these coding agents every day. And we kept running into the same walls:&lt;/p&gt;&lt;ul&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;They only run on your laptop or an expensive VPS:&lt;/b&gt; there&amp;#39;s no sharing, no collaboration, no handoff between devices.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;They&amp;#39;re expensive when idle&lt;/b&gt;: a fixed monthly cost whether the agent is working or not. Scale that to a team, or a company, and it adds up fast.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;They require management and manual setup&lt;/b&gt;: installing dependencies, managing updates, configuring identity and secrets.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;/ul&gt;&lt;p&gt;And there&amp;#39;s a deeper structural issue. Traditional applications serve many users from one instance. As mentioned in our Welcome to Agents Week post, &lt;a href=\&quot;https://blog.cloudflare.com/welcome-to-agents-week/\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;agents are one-to-one&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;. Each agent is a unique instance, serving one user, running one task. A restaurant has a menu and a kitchen optimized to churn out dishes at volume. An agent is more like a personal chef: different ingredients, different techniques, different tools every time.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;That fundamentally changes the scaling math. If a hundred million knowledge workers each use an agentic assistant at even modest concurrency, you need capacity for tens of millions of simultaneous sessions. At current per-container costs, that&amp;#39;s unsustainable. We need a different foundation.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;That&amp;#39;s what we&amp;#39;ve been building.&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h2 id=\&quot;introducing-project-think\&quot;&gt;Introducing Project Think&lt;/h2&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#introducing-project-think\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;Project Think ships a set of new primitives for the Agents SDK:&lt;/p&gt;&lt;ul&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;Durable execution&lt;/b&gt; with fibers: crash recovery, checkpointing, automatic keepalive&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;Sub-agents&lt;/b&gt;: isolated child agents with their own SQLite and typed RPC&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;Persistent sessions&lt;/b&gt;: tree-structured messages, forking, compaction, full-text search&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;Sandboxed code execution&lt;/b&gt;: Dynamic Workers, codemode, runtime npm resolution&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;The execution ladder&lt;/b&gt;: workspace, isolate, npm, browser, sandbox&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;Self-authored extensions&lt;/b&gt;: agents that write their own tools at runtime&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;/ul&gt;&lt;p&gt;Each of these is usable directly with the Agent base class. Build exactly what you need with the primitives, or use the Think base class to get started fast. Let&amp;#39;s look at what each one does.&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h2 id=\&quot;long-running-agents\&quot;&gt;Long-running agents&lt;/h2&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#long-running-agents\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;Agents, as they exist today, are ephemeral. They run for a session, tied to a single process or device, and then they are gone. A coding agent that dies when your laptop sleeps, thatâs a tool. An agent that persists â that can wake up on demand, continue work after interruptions, and carry forward the state without depending on your local runtime â that starts to look like infrastructure. And it changes the scaling model for agents completely.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;The Agents SDK builds on &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/durable-objects/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Durable Objects&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; to give every agent an identity, persistent state, and the ability to wake on message. This is the &lt;a href=\&quot;https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Actor_model\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;actor model&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;: each agent is an addressable entity with its own SQLite database. It consumes zero compute when hibernated. When something happens (an HTTP request, a WebSocket message, a scheduled alarm, an inbound email) the platform wakes the agent, loads its state, and hands it the event. The agent does its work, then goes back to sleep.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;table&gt;&lt;tr&gt;&lt;th&gt;&lt;p&gt;\n&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/th&gt;&lt;th&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;VMs / Containers&lt;/b&gt;&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/th&gt;&lt;th&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;Durable Objects&lt;/b&gt;&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/th&gt;&lt;/tr&gt;&lt;tr&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;Idle cost&lt;/b&gt;&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;p&gt;Full compute cost, always&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;p&gt;Zero (hibernated)&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;/tr&gt;&lt;tr&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;Scaling&lt;/b&gt;&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;p&gt;Provision and manage capacity&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;p&gt;Automatic, per-agent&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;/tr&gt;&lt;tr&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;State&lt;/b&gt;&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;p&gt;External database required&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;p&gt;Built-in SQLite&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;/tr&gt;&lt;tr&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;Recovery&lt;/b&gt;&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;p&gt;You build it (process managers, health checks)&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;p&gt;Platform restarts, state survives&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;/tr&gt;&lt;tr&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;Identity / routing&lt;/b&gt;&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;p&gt;You build it (load balancers, sticky sessions)&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;p&gt;Built-in (name â agent)&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;/tr&gt;&lt;tr&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;10,000 agents, each active 1% of the time&lt;/b&gt;&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;p&gt;10,000 always-on instances&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;p&gt;~100 active at any moment&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;/tr&gt;&lt;/table&gt;&lt;p&gt;This changes the economics of running agents at scale. Instead of &amp;quot;one expensive agent per power user,&amp;quot; you can build &amp;quot;one agent per customer&amp;quot; or &amp;quot;one agent per task&amp;quot; or &amp;quot;one agent per email thread.&amp;quot; The marginal cost of spawning a new agent is effectively zero.&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h3 id=\&quot;surviving-crashes-durable-execution-with-fibers\&quot;&gt;Surviving crashes: durable execution with fibers&lt;/h3&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#surviving-crashes-durable-execution-with-fibers\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;An LLM call takes 30 seconds. A multi-turn agent loop can run for much longer. At any point during that window, the execution environment can vanish: a deploy, a platform restart, hitting resource limits. The upstream connection to the model provider is severed permanently, in-memory state is lost, and connected clients see the stream stop with no explanation.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;code&gt;&lt;/code&gt;&lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/agents/api-reference/durable-execution/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;code&gt;&lt;u&gt;runFiber()&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/a&gt; solves this. A fiber is a durable function invocation: registered in SQLite before execution begins, checkpointable at any point via &lt;code&gt;stash()&lt;/code&gt;, and recoverable on restart via &lt;code&gt;onFiberRecovered&lt;/code&gt;.&lt;/p&gt;\n            &lt;pre class=\&quot;language-Typescript\&quot;&gt;&lt;code class=\&quot;language-Typescript\&quot;&gt;import { Agent } from &amp;quot;agents&amp;quot;;\n\nexport class ResearchAgent extends Agent {\n  async startResearch(topic: string) {\n    void this.runFiber(&amp;quot;research&amp;quot;, async (ctx) =&amp;gt; {\n      const findings = [];\n\n      for (let i = 0; i &amp;lt; 10; i++) {\n        const result = await this.callLLM(`Research step ${i}: ${topic}`);\n        findings.push(result);\n\n        // Checkpoint: if evicted, we resume from here\n        ctx.stash({ findings, step: i, topic });\n\n        this.broadcast({ type: &amp;quot;progress&amp;quot;, step: i });\n      }\n\n      return { findings };\n    });\n  }\n\n  async onFiberRecovered(ctx) {\n    if (ctx.name === &amp;quot;research&amp;quot; &amp;amp;&amp;amp; ctx.snapshot) {\n      const { topic } = ctx.snapshot;\n      await this.startResearch(topic);\n    }\n  }\n}\n&lt;/pre&gt;&lt;/code&gt;\n            &lt;p&gt;The SDK keeps the agent alive automatically during fiber execution, no special configuration needed. For work measured in minutes, keepAlive() / keepAliveWhile() prevents eviction during active work. For longer operations (CI pipelines, design reviews, video generation) the agent starts the work, persists the job ID, hibernates, and wakes on callback.&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h3 id=\&quot;delegating-work-sub-agents-via-facets\&quot;&gt;Delegating work: sub-agents via Facets&lt;/h3&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#delegating-work-sub-agents-via-facets\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;A single agent shouldn&amp;#39;t do everything itself. &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/agents/api-reference/sub-agents/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Sub-agents&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; are child Durable Objects colocated with the parent via &lt;a href=\&quot;https://blog.cloudflare.com/durable-object-facets-dynamic-workers/\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Facets&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;, each with their own isolated SQLite and execution context:&lt;/p&gt;\n            &lt;pre class=\&quot;language-Typescript\&quot;&gt;&lt;code class=\&quot;language-Typescript\&quot;&gt;import { Agent } from &amp;quot;agents&amp;quot;;\n\nexport class ResearchAgent extends Agent {\n  async search(query: string) { /* ... */ }\n}\n\nexport class ReviewAgent extends Agent {\n  async analyze(query: string) { /* ... */ }\n}\n\nexport class Orchestrator extends Agent {\n  async handleTask(task: string) {\n    const researcher = await this.subAgent(ResearchAgent, &amp;quot;research&amp;quot;);\n    const reviewer = await this.subAgent(ReviewAgent, &amp;quot;review&amp;quot;);\n\n    const [research, review] = await Promise.all([\n      researcher.search(task),\n      reviewer.analyze(task)\n    ]);\n\n    return this.synthesize(research, review);\n  }\n}\n&lt;/pre&gt;&lt;/code&gt;\n            &lt;p&gt;Sub-agents are isolated at the storage level. Each one gets its own SQLite database, and thereâs no implicit sharing of data between them. This is enforced by the runtime where sub-agent RPC latency is a function call. TypeScript catches misuse at compile time.&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h3 id=\&quot;conversations-that-persist-the-session-api\&quot;&gt;Conversations that persist: the Session API&lt;/h3&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#conversations-that-persist-the-session-api\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;Agents that run for days or weeks need more than the typical flat list of messages. The experimental &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/agents/api-reference/sessions/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Session API&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; models this explicitly. Available on the Agent base class, conversations are stored as trees, where each message has a parent_id. This enables forking (explore an alternative without losing the original path), non-destructive compaction (summarize older messages rather than deleting them), and full-text search across conversation history via &lt;a href=\&quot;https://www.sqlite.org/fts5.html\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;FTS5&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/p&gt;\n            &lt;pre class=\&quot;language-Typescript\&quot;&gt;&lt;code class=\&quot;language-Typescript\&quot;&gt;import { Agent } from &amp;quot;agents&amp;quot;;\nimport { Session, SessionManager } from &amp;quot;agents/experimental/memory/session&amp;quot;;\n\nexport class MyAgent extends Agent {\n  sessions = SessionManager.create(this);\n\n  async onStart() {\n    const session = this.sessions.create(&amp;quot;main&amp;quot;);\n    const history = session.getHistory();\n    const forked = this.sessions.fork(session.id, messageId, &amp;quot;alternative-approach&amp;quot;);\n  }\n}\n&lt;/pre&gt;&lt;/code&gt;\n            &lt;p&gt;Session is usable directly with &lt;code&gt;Agent&lt;/code&gt;, and it&amp;#39;s the storage layer that the &lt;code&gt;Think&lt;/code&gt; base class builds on.&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h2 id=\&quot;from-tool-calls-to-code-execution\&quot;&gt;From tool calls to code execution&lt;/h2&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#from-tool-calls-to-code-execution\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;Conventional tool-calling has an awkward shape. The model calls a tool, pulls the result back through the context window, calls another tool, pulls that back, and so on. As the tool surface grows, this gets both expensive and clumsy. A hundred files means a hundred round-trips through the model.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;But &lt;a href=\&quot;https://blog.cloudflare.com/code-mode/\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;models are better at writing code to use a system than they are at playing the tool-calling game&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;. This is the insight behind &lt;a href=\&quot;https://github.com/cloudflare/agents/tree/main/packages/codemode\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;@cloudflare/codemode&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;: instead of sequential tool calls, the LLM writes a single program that handles the entire task.&lt;/p&gt;\n            &lt;pre class=\&quot;language-Typescript\&quot;&gt;&lt;code class=\&quot;language-Typescript\&quot;&gt;// The LLM writes this. It runs in a sandboxed Dynamic Worker.\nconst files = await tools.find({ pattern: &amp;quot;**/*.ts&amp;quot; });\nconst results = [];\nfor (const file of files) {\n  const content = await tools.read({ path: file });\n  if (content.includes(&amp;quot;TODO&amp;quot;)) {\n    results.push({ file, todos: content.match(/\\/\\/ TODO:.*/g) });\n  }\n}\nreturn results;\n&lt;/pre&gt;&lt;/code&gt;\n            &lt;p&gt;Instead of 100 round-trips to the model, you just run a single program. This leads to fewer tokens used, faster execution, and better results. The &lt;a href=\&quot;https://github.com/cloudflare/mcp\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Cloudflare API MCP server&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; demonstrates this at scale. We expose only two tools &lt;code&gt;(search()&lt;/code&gt; and &lt;code&gt;execute())&lt;/code&gt;, which consume ~1,000 tokens, vs. ~1.17 million tokens for the naive tool-per-endpoint equivalent. This is a 99.9% reduction.&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h3 id=\&quot;the-missing-primitive-safe-sandboxes\&quot;&gt;The missing primitive: safe sandboxes&lt;/h3&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#the-missing-primitive-safe-sandboxes\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;Once you accept that models should write code on behalf of users, the question becomes: where does that code run? Not eventually, not after a product team turns it into a roadmap item. Right now, for this user, against this system, with tightly defined permissions.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;a href=\&quot;https://blog.cloudflare.com/dynamic-workers/\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Dynamic Workers&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; are that sandbox. A fresh V8 isolate spun up at runtime, in milliseconds, with a few megabytes of memory. That&amp;#39;s roughly 100x faster and up to 100x more memory-efficient than a container. You can start a new one for every single request, run a snippet of code, and throw it away.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;The critical design choice is the capability model. Instead of starting with a general-purpose machine and trying to constrain it, Dynamic Workers begin with almost no ambient authority (&lt;code&gt;globalOutbound: null&lt;/code&gt;, no network access) and the developer grants capabilities explicitly, resource by resource, through bindings. We go from asking &amp;quot;how do we stop this thing from doing too much?&amp;quot; to &amp;quot;what exactly do we want this thing to be able to do?&amp;quot;&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;This is the right question for agent infrastructure.&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h3 id=\&quot;the-execution-ladder\&quot;&gt;The execution ladder&lt;/h3&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#the-execution-ladder\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;This capability model leads naturally to a spectrum of compute environments, an &lt;b&gt;execution ladder&lt;/b&gt; that the agent escalates through as needed:&lt;/p&gt;\n          &lt;figure class=\&quot;kg-card kg-image-card\&quot;&gt;\n          &lt;Image src=\&quot;https://cf-assets.www.cloudflare.com/zkvhlag99gkb/6yokfTVcg8frH4snf7c4sp/2306d721650b4956b28e2198f7cf915d/BLOG-3200_2.png\&quot; alt=\&quot;BLOG-3200 2\&quot; class=\&quot;kg-image\&quot; width=\&quot;1288\&quot; height=\&quot;1202\&quot; loading=\&quot;lazy\&quot;/&gt;\n          &lt;/figure&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;Tier 0&lt;/b&gt; is the Workspace, a durable virtual filesystem backed by SQLite and R2. Read, write, edit, search, grep, diff. Powered by &lt;a href=\&quot;https://www.npmjs.com/package/@cloudflare/shell\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;code&gt;&lt;u&gt;@cloudflare/shell&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;Tier 1&lt;/b&gt; is a Dynamic Worker: LLM-generated JavaScript running in a sandboxed isolate with no network access. Powered by &lt;a href=\&quot;https://www.npmjs.com/package/@cloudflare/codemode\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;code&gt;&lt;u&gt;@cloudflare/codemode&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;Tier 2&lt;/b&gt; adds npm. &lt;a href=\&quot;https://github.com/cloudflare/agents/tree/main/packages/worker-bundler\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;code&gt;&lt;u&gt;@cloudflare/worker-bundler&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/a&gt; fetches packages from the registry, bundles them with esbuild, and loads the result into the Dynamic Worker. The agent writes &lt;code&gt;import { z } from &amp;quot;zod&amp;quot;&lt;/code&gt; and it just works.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;Tier 3&lt;/b&gt; is a headless browser via &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/browser-rendering/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Cloudflare Browser Run&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;. Navigate, click, extract, screenshot. Useful when the service doesn&amp;#39;t support agents yet via MCP or APIs.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;Tier 4&lt;/b&gt; is a &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/sandbox/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Cloudflare Sandbox&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; configured with your toolchains, repos, and dependencies: &lt;code&gt;git clone, npm test, cargo build&lt;/code&gt;, synced bidirectionally with the Workspace.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;The key design principle: &lt;b&gt;the agent should be useful at Tier 0 alone, where each tier is additive.&lt;/b&gt; The user can add capabilities as they go.&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h3 id=\&quot;building-blocks-not-a-framework\&quot;&gt;Building blocks, not a framework&lt;/h3&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#building-blocks-not-a-framework\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;All of these primitives are available as standalone packages. &lt;a href=\&quot;https://blog.cloudflare.com/dynamic-workers/\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Dynamic Workers&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;, &lt;a href=\&quot;https://github.com/cloudflare/agents/tree/main/packages/codemode\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;code&gt;&lt;u&gt;@cloudflare/codemode&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/a&gt;, &lt;a href=\&quot;https://github.com/cloudflare/agents/tree/main/packages/worker-bundler\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;code&gt;&lt;u&gt;@cloudflare/worker-bundler&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/a&gt;, and &lt;a href=\&quot;https://www.npmjs.com/package/@cloudflare/shell\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;code&gt;&lt;u&gt;@cloudflare/shell&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/a&gt; (a durable filesystem with tools) are all usable directly with the Agent base class. You can combine them to give any agent a workspace, code execution, and runtime package resolution without adopting an opinionated framework.&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h2 id=\&quot;the-platform\&quot;&gt;The platform&lt;/h2&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#the-platform\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;Here&amp;#39;s the complete stack for building agents on Cloudflare:&lt;/p&gt;&lt;table&gt;&lt;tr&gt;&lt;th&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;Capability&lt;/b&gt;&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/th&gt;&lt;th&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;What it does&lt;/b&gt;&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/th&gt;&lt;th&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;Powered by&lt;/b&gt;&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/th&gt;&lt;/tr&gt;&lt;tr&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;p&gt;Per-agent isolation&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;p&gt;Every agent is its own world&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/durable-objects/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Durable Objects&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; (DOs)&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;/tr&gt;&lt;tr&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;p&gt;Zero cost when idle&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;p&gt;$0 until the agent wakes up&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/durable-objects/best-practices/websockets/#websocket-hibernation-api\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;DO Hibernation&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;/tr&gt;&lt;tr&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;p&gt;Persistent state&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;p&gt;Queryable, transactional storage&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/durable-objects/best-practices/access-durable-objects-storage/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;DO SQLite&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;/tr&gt;&lt;tr&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;p&gt;Durable filesystem&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;p&gt;Files that survive restarts&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;p&gt;Workspace (SQLite + &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/r2/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;R2&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;)&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;/tr&gt;&lt;tr&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;p&gt;Sandboxed code execution&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;p&gt;Run LLM-generated code safely&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;a href=\&quot;https://blog.cloudflare.com/dynamic-workers/\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Dynamic Workers&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; + &lt;a href=\&quot;https://github.com/cloudflare/agents/tree/main/packages/codemode\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;code&gt;&lt;u&gt;@cloudflare/codemode&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;/tr&gt;&lt;tr&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;p&gt;Runtime dependencies&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;code&gt;import * from react&lt;/code&gt; just works&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;a href=\&quot;https://github.com/cloudflare/agents/tree/main/packages/worker-bundler\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;code&gt;&lt;u&gt;@cloudflare/worker-bundler&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;/tr&gt;&lt;tr&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;p&gt;Web automation&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;p&gt;Browse, navigate, fill forms&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/browser-rendering/\&quot;
